# SquareTool Comprehensive Code Review Question Bank

This question bank is based on the implementation reference in `PROJECT(8).md`. It is designed for reviewing the entire current SquareTool Android project one focused task at a time.

Each question is intentionally self-contained. The complete anti-hallucination, evidence, minimal-change, and simplicity instructions are repeated inside every question so that any single question can be copied directly into Codex without relying on instructions elsewhere in this file.

Total review questions: **460**

## Section 1: Review foundations and product boundary
<a id="section-1-review-foundations-and-product-boundary"></a>

### Q001: Verify the offline-only product boundary against the actual production artifact

Review the current SquareTool checkout for the following task: **Verify the offline-only product boundary against the actual production artifact**. Confirm that the release application contains no account, backend, cloud sync, collaboration, analytics, advertising, billing, subscription, AI, remote image, HTTP client, WebView, or other networked behavior that contradicts the stated SquareTool scope. Inspect source usage as well as merged manifests and the resolved release runtime graph, because an unused declaration and a reachable production path are different. Identify any transitive SDK that initializes itself or collects data even when the app code does not call it.

Follow the complete relevant path through the whole repository, all build variants, generated manifests, runtime dependencies, source sets, resources, tests, and release configuration. Compare the actual source, resources, Gradle configuration, exported Room schema, generated artifacts, and executable tests with PROJECT.md when they are relevant, but treat the current checkout as authoritative. Record the exact files, classes, functions, queries, states, and tests inspected. Where source review cannot prove runtime behavior, identify and run or specify the smallest exact device or emulator scenario instead of claiming that source inspection proves it.

Review discipline: AI reviewers can hallucinate, misread intent, or invent defects. Do not assume this area is broken, and do not force a finding. It is completely acceptable, and often preferable, to conclude that the implementation is correct and make no changes. Verify the current checkout rather than relying only on PROJECT.md, and trace the complete relevant execution, state, persistence, and UI path before reaching a conclusion. Base every finding on concrete code, configuration, test evidence, or a reproducible state sequence. Clearly distinguish a proven defect from a theoretical risk, optional hardening, or stylistic preference. Do not make speculative, cosmetic, preference-only, or unrelated changes. Preserve intentional behavior and existing user-visible contracts. If a real issue is proven, apply only the smallest safe correction and add or adjust the smallest meaningful regression test when practical. Keep the code clean, direct, and easy to understand. Less is better: do not add abstractions, helper layers, wrappers, dependencies, defensive branches, comments, or generalized frameworks unless they are necessary to solve the demonstrated problem. Do not refactor working code merely to make it different. If no correction is genuinely needed, explicitly report that the reviewed area is sound and leave the code unchanged.

Return one of two outcomes. Use `No change needed` when the implementation is sound, and name the files, flows, and evidence you checked. Use `Proven issue` only when you can show the exact affected code, triggering state or input sequence, violated invariant, practical impact, smallest safe fix, and the validation or regression test that proves the correction. Do not edit unrelated code.

### Q003: Verify the supported 1 to 50 by 1 to 50 grid boundary everywhere

Review the current SquareTool checkout for the following task: **Verify the supported 1 to 50 by 1 to 50 grid boundary everywhere**. Follow grid dimensions from every UI entry point through draft parsing, ViewModel state, domain models, repository validation, Room writes, backup import, generation, rendering, insights, and export. ### Q009: Verify that responsibilities are located in the intended ownership layer

Review the current SquareTool checkout for the following task: **Verify that responsibilities are located in the intended ownership layer**. Inspect whether application startup and routing remain in `app`, Room details in `data/local`, transactions in repositories, pure rules in domain packages, motif code in `render`, file creation in `export`, and serialization in `backup`. Flag a boundary violation only when it creates concrete coupling, duplicated truth, lifecycle risk, or testability problems. Do not move code merely to make the package tree look theoretically perfect.

Follow the complete relevant path through the source ownership map, module boundaries, package structure, source sets, resources, generated outputs, and actual references between app, data, domain, render, UI, export, backup, tests, config, and tools.
